- IN SCOPE: (1) **first, check whether Oklahoma's official candidate/election source runs Civix-vended software** (same URL pattern as `goelect.txelections.civixapps.com` — subdomain of `*.civixapps.com`, "IvisCbpUi"/"Civix Election Night Results" page titles) — if so, read `docs/operations/nationwide-congressional-roster-plan.md`'s "Civix portal operational playbook" section FIRST, before rediscovering its mechanics from scratch; (2) fetch + hand-transcribe Oklahoma's official Secretary of State / State Election Board candidate roster (both US House — all OK districts — and the 2026 US Senate race, if one exists) into `scripts/congressional-rosters/ok-official-roster-2026.ts`, importing shared types from `scripts/congressional-rosters/types.ts` (do not duplicate AZ's or TX's local type definitions); (3) register it in `scripts/ingest/official-roster.ts`'s `FIXTURES` map (already array-shaped per state — TX's entry is the reference for a state with both chambers); (4) apply migration 0016 (if not already applied to the target staging branch — it fixes a real Senate-row idempotency bug found during the TX build) + run the importer against the isolated staging Neon branch — never production; (5) add automated tests mirroring `officialRoster.test.ts`'s AZ/TX coverage; (6) end-to-end verification against staging with the flag on, comparing literal app output candidate-by-candidate against the official source; (7) write `docs/operations/oklahoma-vertical-slice-data-check.md` with the full comparison AND a written operational-navigation section (site structure, filter sequence, which signals proved reliable, tooling used) per the plan doc's strengthened standing requirement.
- OUT OF SCOPE: any state other than Oklahoma; production database writes/migrations; enabling `OFFICIAL_ROSTER_ENABLED` anywhere persistent; the automated parser-family fan-out (paused, see epic); expanding to non-congressional (state legislature) races.
- SAFETY: official-source reads only (no Ballotpedia as a primary source — comparison/spot-check only); all writes go to the isolated staging branch, never production; a blocked/unpublished/ambiguous official source stays explicit, never guessed; **any OK district whose runoff hasn't happened yet (post-2026-08-25) must be recorded as an explicit not-yet-determined state, never inferred from primary-round standings** (this is the exact mistake caught and corrected during the TX build — a primary leader is not the nominee if a runoff is still pending); incumbency must be cross-checked against an independent official source (e.g. `house.gov`'s member directory for House, similar for Senate), never guessed from an unreliable in-portal `(I)` marker, and never sourced from this app's own FEC-derived `candidates` table.
- ATTENDED: yes — same contract as 637c2583/8530a468. Build + verify, then STOP and surface to Muxin for sign-off before any merge or flag flip.
- DELIVERABLE REQUIREMENT (Muxin, 2026-07-15, strengthened after TX): The final report/summary MUST state, in full (absolute file paths, untruncated URLs): (a) the full absolute file path to the doc holding the app's output/comparison (mirrors `docs/operations/texas-vertical-slice-data-check.md`), (b) the full absolute file path to the fixture file holding the transcribed data, (c) the exact, full official Oklahoma source URL(s) used, and (d) a written operational-navigation section — see the plan doc's "Standing verification-deliverable requirement" and "Civix portal operational playbook" sections.
- GOAL_CONDITION: `npm run check` passes; the new state's fixture imports idempotently to staging (verify by direct row-count query, not just the importer's self-reported count — see migration 0016's history); an end-to-end check against staging (flag on) shows the app's literal output matching the official roster for every *determined* contest in that state (runoff-pending districts explicitly excluded/flagged, not guessed), with the comparison + operational-navigation writeup for review, including the file-path/source-URL deliverable above.
- STATUS: Done — code merged to main 2026-07-15, per Muxin's explicit sign-off (manual CSV/data verification against the official results portal satisfied, plus sign-off to merge AZ→TX→OK in sequence). Flag NOT enabled anywhere persistent; no production writes; nothing exposed to real users.
- DEPENDS ON: [P0] Import + verify official roster: Texas (TX) (continue the manual state-by-state track; TX is the most recently proven build, including the shared migration-0016 fix and the strengthened deliverable-requirement pattern this card follows)
- DECISION: attended manual gate — same authorized scope as 637c2583/8530a468, extended to one additional state. No production mutation, no flag flip, no fan-out beyond the chosen state.
- GROOMED: ready: pattern proven twice (AZ, TX); Civix-playbook + strengthened deliverable-requirement infrastructure already in place — 2026-07-15

**[P0] Import + verify official roster: Alabama (AL)**
- PARENT: c5a813bb-9223-4dc1-95aa-65637eb6940b
- ORIGIN: 2026-07-15, Muxin's decision after the OK build: rather than hand-picking each next state for a specific technical gap or ease of manual verification (most states still have primaries/runoffs ahead of them anyway, so there's no clean "already fully decided" state to prefer), continue the manual track in **plain alphabetical order** from here — Alabama is first alphabetically among states not yet built through this track (AZ, TX, OK are done). A Wave-2 rehearsal (F03, `scripts/congressional-rosters/f03-source-inventory.ts`, jurisdiction "AL") already inventoried Alabama's official source as a head start: Alabama Secretary of State, `sourceFormat: "html"`, `parserFamily: "html_table"` (NOT Civix), landing page `https://www.sos.alabama.gov/alabama-votes/voter/election-information/2026`. That inventory's `contestScope.electionDates` lists `2026-05-19` (primary), `2026-06-16` (runoff), `2026-08-11`, and `2026-11-03` (general) — the `2026-08-11` date doesn't fit the regular primary/runoff/general pattern and may indicate a separate special congressional election; **confirm what that date actually is during this build, don't assume**. Per the now-standing epic-level requirement (see the epic card's own "STANDING REQUIREMENT" bullet and the plan doc's Oklahoma revision section), this build must check Alabama's own official source for any still-undetermined nomination and apply the existing `runoff_pending` ballotStatus + `SeatChallenger.isRunoffPending` + RepCard CTA pattern if one is found — do not assume Alabama's regular May/June cycle is fully decided without checking.
- OUTCOME: One more state built and verified end-to-end through the pattern cards 637c2583 (Arizona), 8530a468 (Texas), and d9b1ef86 (Oklahoma) proved: official roster governs the candidate set + incumbent-on-ballot status for that state (House AND Senate, if AL has a 2026 Senate contest — confirm during build, not assumed here) only, flag-gated, zero behavior change elsewhere, verified live against an isolated Neon staging branch before any sign-off.
- IN SCOPE: (1) confirm Alabama's official source is what the F03 rehearsal found (HTML, not Civix) before assuming any pattern from AZ/TX/OK applies as-is; (2) fetch + hand-transcribe Alabama's official Secretary of State candidate roster (both US House — all AL districts — and the 2026 US Senate race, if one exists) into `scripts/congressional-rosters/al-official-roster-2026.ts`, importing shared types from `scripts/congressional-rosters/types.ts` (do not duplicate AZ's/TX's/OK's local type definitions); (3) register it in `scripts/ingest/official-roster.ts`'s `FIXTURES` map (already array-shaped per state); (4) apply any needed migration (check `db/schema.ts`/staging's live index definition first — the OK build found no migration was needed for the existing `runoff_pending` status since `ballot_status` is plain text) + run the importer against the isolated staging Neon branch — never production; (5) add automated tests mirroring `officialRoster.test.ts`'s AZ/TX/OK coverage; (6) end-to-end verification against staging with the flag on, comparing literal app output candidate-by-candidate against the official source; (7) write `docs/operations/alabama-vertical-slice-data-check.md` with the full comparison AND a written operational-navigation section per the plan doc's standing requirement; (8) check for any still-undetermined nomination (see ORIGIN note above) and apply `runoff_pending` + the RepCard CTA if found — this is now a standing requirement for every remaining 2026 state, not optional.
- OUT OF SCOPE: any state other than Alabama; production database writes/migrations; enabling `OFFICIAL_ROSTER_ENABLED` anywhere persistent; the automated parser-family fan-out (paused, see epic); expanding to non-congressional (state legislature) races.
- SAFETY: official-source reads only (no Ballotpedia as a primary source — comparison/spot-check only); all writes go to the isolated staging branch, never production; a blocked/unpublished/ambiguous official source stays explicit, never guessed; any AL district/seat whose nomination isn't yet determined must be recorded as `runoff_pending`, never inferred from partial results; incumbency must be cross-checked against an independent official source (e.g. `house.gov`'s member directory for House, `senate.gov`/Bioguide for Senate — the OK build found the app's own framing of "who holds this Senate seat" can be stale, so never skip this cross-check), never guessed from an unreliable in-portal signal, and never sourced from this app's own FEC-derived `candidates` table.
- ATTENDED: build + verify (candidate-by-candidate against the official source, cross-checked against a second independent source when one exists) + self-vet, then MERGE DIRECTLY — per the epic's "STANDING REQUIREMENT — MERGE PROMPTLY, NO SEPARATE SIGN-OFF GATE" (2026-07-15). This supersedes the earlier "STOP for sign-off before merge" contract 637c2583/8530a468/d9b1ef86 carried. Do not let this branch sit unmerged waiting on Muxin — merge as soon as self-vet is clean. The flag flip (`OFFICIAL_ROSTER_ENABLED` going live for real users) is still a separate, gated decision, unaffected by this.
- DELIVERABLE REQUIREMENT (Muxin, 2026-07-15): The final report/summary MUST state, in full (absolute file paths, untruncated URLs): (a) the full absolute file path to the doc holding the app's output/comparison (mirrors `docs/operations/oklahoma-vertical-slice-data-check.md`), (b) the full absolute file path to the fixture file holding the transcribed data, (c) the exact, full official Alabama source URL(s) used, and (d) a written operational-navigation section — see the plan doc's "Standing verification-deliverable requirement" and "Civix portal operational playbook" sections (the latter only applies if Alabama turns out to run Civix after all, which the F03 rehearsal says it doesn't).
- GOAL_CONDITION: `npm run check` passes; the new state's fixture imports idempotently to staging (verify by direct row-count query, not just the importer's self-reported count); an end-to-end check against staging (flag on) shows the app's literal output matching the official roster for every *determined* contest in that state (any undetermined nomination explicitly flagged as `runoff_pending`, not guessed), with the comparison + operational-navigation writeup for review, including the file-path/source-URL deliverable above.
- STATUS: Done
- DEPENDS ON: [P0] Import + verify official roster: Oklahoma (OK) (continue the manual state-by-state track, now in plain alphabetical order per Muxin's 2026-07-15 decision)
- DECISION: self-vet auto-merge gate — same authorized scope as 637c2583/8530a468/d9b1ef86, extended to one additional state. Build + verify + self-vet, then merge directly (no separate Muxin sign-off before merge, per the epic's 2026-07-15 standing requirement). No production mutation, no flag flip, no fan-out beyond the chosen state.

- OUT OF SCOPE: any jurisdiction other than Indiana; production database writes/migrations; enabling `OFFICIAL_ROSTER_ENABLED` anywhere persistent; the automated parser-family fan-out (paused, see epic — do not confuse this card with the separate "Source inventory" cards on that track); expanding to non-congressional (state legislature) races.
- SAFETY: official-source reads only (no Ballotpedia as a primary source — comparison/spot-check only); all writes go to the isolated staging branch, never production; a blocked/unpublished/ambiguous official source stays explicit, never guessed; any seat whose nomination isn't yet determined must be recorded as `runoff_pending`, never inferred from partial results; incumbency must be cross-checked against an independent official source (e.g. `house.gov`'s member directory for House, `senate.gov`/Bioguide for Senate), never guessed from an unreliable in-portal signal, and never sourced from this app's own FEC-derived `candidates` table.
- ATTENDED: build + verify (candidate-by-candidate against the official source, cross-checked against a second independent source when one exists) + self-vet, then MERGE DIRECTLY — per the epic's "STANDING REQUIREMENT — MERGE PROMPTLY, NO SEPARATE SIGN-OFF GATE" (2026-07-15). Do not let this branch sit unmerged waiting on Muxin — merge as soon as self-vet is clean. The flag flip (`OFFICIAL_ROSTER_ENABLED` going live for real users) is still a separate, gated decision, unaffected by this.

- STATUS: Done
- RESULT: PR #340 merged (`0e3840bb`) 2026-07-16. Indiana is not Civix — the SoS's own "General Election Candidate List" XLSX had a genuine federal-office publication gap (linked to an unrelated state-legislative recount); worked around by pulling certified May 5, 2026 primary results directly from `enr.indianavoters.in.gov` (100% of 5,067 precincts, certified June 1, 2026), cross-checked against house.gov for incumbency. 9 US House districts, no Senate contest in 2026, no `runoff_pending` rows. Verified 0 mismatches against staging with the flag on. See `docs/operations/indiana-vertical-slice-data-check.md`.
- DECISION: self-vet auto-merge gate — same authorized scope as the AZ/TX/OK/AL cards, extended to one additional jurisdiction. Build + verify + self-vet, then merge directly (no separate Muxin sign-off before merge, per the epic's 2026-07-15 standing requirement). No production mutation, no flag flip, no fan-out beyond the chosen jurisdiction. No `DEPENDS ON` another state card — per Muxin's 2026-07-15 clarification, the manual track scopes each *session* to one state at a time, it does not block the *backlog* to one state at a time; any of these cards may be picked up and built in parallel, each from its own worktree cut from current `origin/main`.
- GROOMED: ready: pattern proven four times (AZ, TX, OK, AL); Civix-playbook + strengthened deliverable-requirement + standing pending-election-tracking infrastructure + merge-promptly policy already in place; I06 rehearsal already inventoried this jurisdiction's source — 2026-07-15

- SAFETY: official-source reads only (no Ballotpedia as a primary source — comparison/spot-check only); all writes go to the isolated staging branch, never production; a blocked/unpublished/ambiguous official source stays explicit, never guessed; any seat whose nomination isn't yet determined must be recorded as `runoff_pending`, never inferred from partial results; incumbency must be cross-checked against an independent official source (e.g. `house.gov`'s member directory for House, `senate.gov`/Bioguide for Senate), never guessed from an unreliable in-portal signal, and never sourced from this app's own FEC-derived `candidates` table.
- ATTENDED: build + verify (candidate-by-candidate against the official source, cross-checked against a second independent source when one exists) + self-vet, then MERGE DIRECTLY — per the epic's "STANDING REQUIREMENT — MERGE PROMPTLY, NO SEPARATE SIGN-OFF GATE" (2026-07-15). Do not let this branch sit unmerged waiting on Muxin — merge as soon as self-vet is clean. The flag flip (`OFFICIAL_ROSTER_ENABLED` going live for real users) is still a separate, gated decision, unaffected by this.
- DELIVERABLE REQUIREMENT (Muxin, 2026-07-15): The final report/summary MUST state, in full (absolute file paths, untruncated URLs): (a) the full absolute file path to the doc holding the app's output/comparison (mirrors `docs/operations/oklahoma-vertical-slice-data-check.md`), (b) the full absolute file path to the fixture file holding the transcribed data, (c) the exact, full official Minnesota source URL(s) used, and (d) a written operational-navigation section — see the plan doc's "Standing verification-deliverable requirement" and "Civix portal operational playbook" sections (the latter only applies if Minnesota turns out to run Civix).
- GOAL_CONDITION: `npm run check` passes; the new jurisdiction's fixture imports idempotently to staging (verify by direct row-count query, not just the importer's self-reported count); an end-to-end check against staging (flag on) shows the app's literal output matching the official roster for every *determined* contest (any undetermined nomination explicitly flagged as `runoff_pending`, not guessed), with the comparison + operational-navigation writeup for review, including the file-path/source-URL deliverable above.
- STATUS: Backlog
- DECISION: self-vet auto-merge gate — same authorized scope as the AZ/TX/OK/AL cards, extended to one additional jurisdiction. Build + verify + self-vet, then merge directly (no separate Muxin sign-off before merge, per the epic's 2026-07-15 standing requirement). No production mutation, no flag flip, no fan-out beyond the chosen jurisdiction. No `DEPENDS ON` another state card — per Muxin's 2026-07-15 clarification, the manual track scopes each *session* to one state at a time, it does not block the *backlog* to one state at a time; any of these cards may be picked up and built in parallel, each from its own worktree cut from current `origin/main`.
- GROOMED: ready: pattern proven four times (AZ, TX, OK, AL); Civix-playbook + strengthened deliverable-requirement + standing pending-election-tracking infrastructure + merge-promptly policy already in place; no pre-existing rehearsal data for this jurisdiction — the build's own research establishes the source from scratch, same as AZ/TX/OK originally — 2026-07-15

No Wave-2/3 rehearsal (F03/I06/I11) covered Nevada — this build's own research must first establish the official election authority's site, format, and whether it runs Civix-vended software (check the URL pattern against `docs/operations/nationwide-congressional-roster-plan.md`'s "Civix portal operational playbook" before assuming either way), exactly as AZ, TX, and OK each did from a cold start.
- OUTCOME: One more jurisdiction built and verified end-to-end through the pattern the AZ, TX, OK, and AL cards proved: official roster governs the candidate set + incumbent-on-ballot status for both US House — all NV districts — and the 2026 US Senate race, if one exists, flag-gated, zero behavior change elsewhere, verified live against an isolated Neon staging branch before merge.
- IN SCOPE: (1) confirm Nevada's official source format/access pattern before assuming any prior state's pattern applies as-is (Civix-vended portal vs. static HTML/PDF/XLSX — check first, per ORIGIN above); (2) fetch + hand-transcribe Nevada's official Secretary of State / State Election Authority candidate roster (both US House — all NV districts — and the 2026 US Senate race, if one exists) into `scripts/congressional-rosters/nv-official-roster-2026.ts`, importing shared types from `scripts/congressional-rosters/types.ts` (do not duplicate any other state's local type definitions); (3) register it in `scripts/ingest/official-roster.ts`'s `FIXTURES` map (already array-shaped per jurisdiction); (4) apply any needed migration (check `db/schema.ts`/staging's live index definition first — no migration has been needed since 0016) + run the importer against the isolated staging Neon branch — never production; (5) add automated tests mirroring `officialRoster.test.ts`'s existing AZ/TX/OK/AL coverage; (6) end-to-end verification against staging with the flag on, comparing literal app output candidate-by-candidate against the official source; (7) write `docs/operations/nevada-vertical-slice-data-check.md` with the full comparison AND a written operational-navigation section per the plan doc's standing requirement; (8) check for any still-undetermined nomination (a pending runoff, an unfinalized primary, etc.) and apply the existing `runoff_pending` ballotStatus + `SeatChallenger.isRunoffPending` + RepCard CTA pattern if found — this is a standing requirement for every remaining state, not optional.
- OUT OF SCOPE: any jurisdiction other than Nevada; production database writes/migrations; enabling `OFFICIAL_ROSTER_ENABLED` anywhere persistent; the automated parser-family fan-out (paused, see epic — do not confuse this card with the separate "Source inventory" cards on that track); expanding to non-congressional (state legislature) races.
- SAFETY: official-source reads only (no Ballotpedia as a primary source — comparison/spot-check only); all writes go to the isolated staging branch, never production; a blocked/unpublished/ambiguous official source stays explicit, never guessed; any seat whose nomination isn't yet determined must be recorded as `runoff_pending`, never inferred from partial results; incumbency must be cross-checked against an independent official source (e.g. `house.gov`'s member directory for House, `senate.gov`/Bioguide for Senate), never guessed from an unreliable in-portal signal, and never sourced from this app's own FEC-derived `candidates` table.
- ATTENDED: build + verify (candidate-by-candidate against the official source, cross-checked against a second independent source when one exists) + self-vet, then MERGE DIRECTLY — per the epic's "STANDING REQUIREMENT — MERGE PROMPTLY, NO SEPARATE SIGN-OFF GATE" (2026-07-15). Do not let this branch sit unmerged waiting on Muxin — merge as soon as self-vet is clean. The flag flip (`OFFICIAL_ROSTER_ENABLED` going live for real users) is still a separate, gated decision, unaffected by this.
- DELIVERABLE REQUIREMENT (Muxin, 2026-07-15): The final report/summary MUST state, in full (absolute file paths, untruncated URLs): (a) the full absolute file path to the doc holding the app's output/comparison (mirrors `docs/operations/oklahoma-vertical-slice-data-check.md`), (b) the full absolute file path to the fixture file holding the transcribed data, (c) the exact, full official Nevada source URL(s) used, and (d) a written operational-navigation section — see the plan doc's "Standing verification-deliverable requirement" and "Civix portal operational playbook" sections (the latter only applies if Nevada turns out to run Civix).
- GOAL_CONDITION: `npm run check` passes; the new jurisdiction's fixture imports idempotently to staging (verify by direct row-count query, not just the importer's self-reported count); an end-to-end check against staging (flag on) shows the app's literal output matching the official roster for every *determined* contest (any undetermined nomination explicitly flagged as `runoff_pending`, not guessed), with the comparison + operational-navigation writeup for review, including the file-path/source-URL deliverable above.
- STATUS: Backlog
- DECISION: self-vet auto-merge gate — same authorized scope as the AZ/TX/OK/AL cards, extended to one additional jurisdiction. Build + verify + self-vet, then merge directly (no separate Muxin sign-off before merge, per the epic's 2026-07-15 standing requirement). No production mutation, no flag flip, no fan-out beyond the chosen jurisdiction. No `DEPENDS ON` another state card — per Muxin's 2026-07-15 clarification, the manual track scopes each *session* to one state at a time, it does not block the *backlog* to one state at a time; any of these cards may be picked up and built in parallel, each from its own worktree cut from current `origin/main`.
- GROOMED: ready: pattern proven four times (AZ, TX, OK, AL); Civix-playbook + strengthened deliverable-requirement + standing pending-election-tracking infrastructure + merge-promptly policy already in place; no pre-existing rehearsal data for this jurisdiction — the build's own research establishes the source from scratch, same as AZ/TX/OK originally — 2026-07-15

- IN SCOPE: (1) confirm North Carolina's official source format/access pattern before assuming any prior state's pattern applies as-is (Civix-vended portal vs. static HTML/PDF/XLSX — check first, per ORIGIN above); (2) fetch + hand-transcribe North Carolina's official Secretary of State / State Election Authority candidate roster (both US House — all NC districts — and the 2026 US Senate race, if one exists) into `scripts/congressional-rosters/nc-official-roster-2026.ts`, importing shared types from `scripts/congressional-rosters/types.ts` (do not duplicate any other state's local type definitions); (3) register it in `scripts/ingest/official-roster.ts`'s `FIXTURES` map (already array-shaped per jurisdiction); (4) apply any needed migration (check `db/schema.ts`/staging's live index definition first — no migration has been needed since 0016) + run the importer against the isolated staging Neon branch — never production; (5) add automated tests mirroring `officialRoster.test.ts`'s existing AZ/TX/OK/AL coverage; (6) end-to-end verification against staging with the flag on, comparing literal app output candidate-by-candidate against the official source; (7) write `docs/operations/north-carolina-vertical-slice-data-check.md` with the full comparison AND a written operational-navigation section per the plan doc's standing requirement; (8) check for any still-undetermined nomination (a pending runoff, an unfinalized primary, etc.) and apply the existing `runoff_pending` ballotStatus + `SeatChallenger.isRunoffPending` + RepCard CTA pattern if found — this is a standing requirement for every remaining state, not optional.
- OUT OF SCOPE: any jurisdiction other than North Carolina; production database writes/migrations; enabling `OFFICIAL_ROSTER_ENABLED` anywhere persistent; the automated parser-family fan-out (paused, see epic — do not confuse this card with the separate "Source inventory" cards on that track); expanding to non-congressional (state legislature) races.

- SAFETY: official-source reads only (no Ballotpedia as a primary source — comparison/spot-check only); all writes go to the isolated staging branch, never production; a blocked/unpublished/ambiguous official source stays explicit, never guessed; any seat whose nomination isn't yet determined must be recorded as `runoff_pending`, never inferred from partial results; incumbency must be cross-checked against an independent official source (e.g. `house.gov`'s member directory for House, `senate.gov`/Bioguide for Senate), never guessed from an unreliable in-portal signal, and never sourced from this app's own FEC-derived `candidates` table.
- ATTENDED: build + verify (candidate-by-candidate against the official source, cross-checked against a second independent source when one exists) + self-vet, then MERGE DIRECTLY — per the epic's "STANDING REQUIREMENT — MERGE PROMPTLY, NO SEPARATE SIGN-OFF GATE" (2026-07-15). Do not let this branch sit unmerged waiting on Muxin — merge as soon as self-vet is clean. The flag flip (`OFFICIAL_ROSTER_ENABLED` going live for real users) is still a separate, gated decision, unaffected by this.
- DELIVERABLE REQUIREMENT (Muxin, 2026-07-15): The final report/summary MUST state, in full (absolute file paths, untruncated URLs): (a) the full absolute file path to the doc holding the app's output/comparison (mirrors `docs/operations/oklahoma-vertical-slice-data-check.md`), (b) the full absolute file path to the fixture file holding the transcribed data, (c) the exact, full official Utah source URL(s) used, and (d) a written operational-navigation section — see the plan doc's "Standing verification-deliverable requirement" and "Civix portal operational playbook" sections (the latter only applies if Utah turns out to run Civix).

A I11 rehearsal (`scripts/congressional-rosters/i11-source-inventory.ts`, jurisdiction "WI") already inventoried Wisconsin's official source as a head start: `sourceFormat: "pdf"`, `parserFamily: "text_pdf"`, landing/artifact URL `https://elections.wi.gov/sites/default/files/documents/D.%20Ballot%20Access%20Report%206.9.2026.pdf`. Confirm this is still current during the build — the rehearsal is a starting point, not a substitute for a live check.
- OUTCOME: One more jurisdiction built and verified end-to-end through the pattern the AZ, TX, OK, and AL cards proved: official roster governs the candidate set + incumbent-on-ballot status for both US House — all WI districts — and the 2026 US Senate race, if one exists, flag-gated, zero behavior change elsewhere, verified live against an isolated Neon staging branch before merge.
- IN SCOPE: (1) confirm Wisconsin's official source format/access pattern before assuming any prior state's pattern applies as-is (Civix-vended portal vs. static HTML/PDF/XLSX — check first, per ORIGIN above); (2) fetch + hand-transcribe Wisconsin's official Secretary of State / State Election Authority candidate roster (both US House — all WI districts — and the 2026 US Senate race, if one exists) into `scripts/congressional-rosters/wi-official-roster-2026.ts`, importing shared types from `scripts/congressional-rosters/types.ts` (do not duplicate any other state's local type definitions); (3) register it in `scripts/ingest/official-roster.ts`'s `FIXTURES` map (already array-shaped per jurisdiction); (4) apply any needed migration (check `db/schema.ts`/staging's live index definition first — no migration has been needed since 0016) + run the importer against the isolated staging Neon branch — never production; (5) add automated tests mirroring `officialRoster.test.ts`'s existing AZ/TX/OK/AL coverage; (6) end-to-end verification against staging with the flag on, comparing literal app output candidate-by-candidate against the official source; (7) write `docs/operations/wisconsin-vertical-slice-data-check.md` with the full comparison AND a written operational-navigation section per the plan doc's standing requirement; (8) check for any still-undetermined nomination (a pending runoff, an unfinalized primary, etc.) and apply the existing `runoff_pending` ballotStatus + `SeatChallenger.isRunoffPending` + RepCard CTA pattern if found — this is a standing requirement for every remaining state, not optional.
- OUT OF SCOPE: any jurisdiction other than Wisconsin; production database writes/migrations; enabling `OFFICIAL_ROSTER_ENABLED` anywhere persistent; the automated parser-family fan-out (paused, see epic — do not confuse this card with the separate "Source inventory" cards on that track); expanding to non-congressional (state legislature) races.
- SAFETY: official-source reads only (no Ballotpedia as a primary source — comparison/spot-check only); all writes go to the isolated staging branch, never production; a blocked/unpublished/ambiguous official source stays explicit, never guessed; any seat whose nomination isn't yet determined must be recorded as `runoff_pending`, never inferred from partial results; incumbency must be cross-checked against an independent official source (e.g. `house.gov`'s member directory for House, `senate.gov`/Bioguide for Senate), never guessed from an unreliable in-portal signal, and never sourced from this app's own FEC-derived `candidates` table.
- ATTENDED: build + verify (candidate-by-candidate against the official source, cross-checked against a second independent source when one exists) + self-vet, then MERGE DIRECTLY — per the epic's "STANDING REQUIREMENT — MERGE PROMPTLY, NO SEPARATE SIGN-OFF GATE" (2026-07-15). Do not let this branch sit unmerged waiting on Muxin — merge as soon as self-vet is clean. The flag flip (`OFFICIAL_ROSTER_ENABLED` going live for real users) is still a separate, gated decision, unaffected by this.
- DELIVERABLE REQUIREMENT (Muxin, 2026-07-15): The final report/summary MUST state, in full (absolute file paths, untruncated URLs): (a) the full absolute file path to the doc holding the app's output/comparison (mirrors `docs/operations/oklahoma-vertical-slice-data-check.md`), (b) the full absolute file path to the fixture file holding the transcribed data, (c) the exact, full official Wisconsin source URL(s) used, and (d) a written operational-navigation section — see the plan doc's "Standing verification-deliverable requirement" and "Civix portal operational playbook" sections (the latter only applies if Wisconsin turns out to run Civix).
- GOAL_CONDITION: `npm run check` passes; the new jurisdiction's fixture imports idempotently to staging (verify by direct row-count query, not just the importer's self-reported count); an end-to-end check against staging (flag on) shows the app's literal output matching the official roster for every *determined* contest (any undetermined nomination explicitly flagged as `runoff_pending`, not guessed), with the comparison + operational-navigation writeup for review, including the file-path/source-URL deliverable above.
- STATUS: Backlog
- DECISION: self-vet auto-merge gate — same authorized scope as the AZ/TX/OK/AL cards, extended to one additional jurisdiction. Build + verify + self-vet, then merge directly (no separate Muxin sign-off before merge, per the epic's 2026-07-15 standing requirement). No production mutation, no flag flip, no fan-out beyond the chosen jurisdiction. No `DEPENDS ON` another state card — per Muxin's 2026-07-15 clarification, the manual track scopes each *session* to one state at a time, it does not block the *backlog* to one state at a time; any of these cards may be picked up and built in parallel, each from its own worktree cut from current `origin/main`.
- GROOMED: ready: pattern proven four times (AZ, TX, OK, AL); Civix-playbook + strengthened deliverable-requirement + standing pending-election-tracking infrastructure + merge-promptly policy already in place; I11 rehearsal already inventoried this jurisdiction's source — 2026-07-15

**[P1] Bump Next.js 15.5.12 -> 15.5.20+ (patches 6 high-severity CVEs)**
- npm audit --omit=dev flags 6 high-severity Next.js advisories at installed 15.5.12: connection-exhaustion DoS in Cache Components (GHSA-mg66-mrh9-m8jx), Image Optimization API DoS (GHSA-h64f-5h5j-jqjh), WebSocket-upgrade SSRF (GHSA-c4j6-fc7j-m34r), dynamic-route-param middleware/proxy bypass (GHSA-492v-c6pp-mqqv), RSC cache poisoning (GHSA-wfc6-r584-vfw7), segment-prefetch + i18n middleware bypass (GHSA-267c-6grr-h53f, GHSA-36qx-fr4f-26g5).
- All 7 advisories list first_patched_version 15.5.16 (confirmed via GitHub Advisory API); 15.5.20 is the latest 15.x release available (dist-tag 'backport') -- a same-major patch bump, no v16 migration needed.
- ORIGIN: idea-scout 2026-07-14 -- dependency-security-advisory
- NEEDS DECISION: machine-invented candidate; explicit human promotion required
- EVIDENCE: https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-mg66-mrh9-m8jx (+6 sibling GHSA ids); local `npm audit --omit=dev` output (retrieved 2026-07-14)
- HYPOTHESIS: same-major patch bump (15.5.12 -> 15.5.20) should be low-risk and closes all 6 flagged CVEs; uncertainty -- unverified whether the 15.5.13-15.5.20 patch range carries any breaking change for this app's usage (Cache Components / middleware / i18n); confirm with a full build+test pass before merge.
- GOAL_CONDITION: npm audit --omit=dev reports zero Next.js advisories after the bump; npm run build and the full test suite pass at the new pinned version.
- STATUS: Backlog
<!-- card-id: 06e9e179-edf9-45c7-a030-c8a9515b9d72 -->

**[idea] Evaluate whether ballot chat should hard-redirect election-logistics questions instead of answering via LLM**
- Current chat surface (docs/BALLOT_PROMPT.md / theme-extraction) is scoped to issue-alignment conversation; it isn't confirmed whether the prompt explicitly refuses/redirects a voter who asks a logistics question ("where do I vote", "when does registration close") to an official source rather than answering from the model's own knowledge.
- External signal: a 2026 Demos/Electoral Commission evaluation found AI chatbots gave inaccurate answers on ~34% of election-procedural test questions in a Scottish-election study; bipartisan US lawmakers and the NY Attorney General have since publicly warned against relying on chatbots for voting/election-logistics answers ahead of the 2026 midterms.
- ORIGIN: idea-scout 2026-07-14 -- external-research/regulatory-industry-signal
- NEEDS DECISION: machine-invented candidate; explicit human promotion required
- EVIDENCE: https://www.resultsense.com/news/2026-05-21-demos-electoral-commission-ai-chatbots-scottish-election/ ; https://ag.ny.gov/publications/protecting-new-york-voters-ai-generated-election-misinformation (retrieved 2026-07-14)
- HYPOTHESIS: a small, bounded prompt/guard change routing logistics questions to the app's own Google-Civic-backed data (or an explicit official-source redirect) would close a plausible-but-unverified gap; uncertainty -- not yet confirmed the current prompt actually mishandles a logistics question in practice, and product scope may already exclude this by design; a manual eval should run before any code change.
- GOAL_CONDITION: a short manual eval (N sample logistics questions run through the live chat) either confirms current behavior is already safe (card closes as not-needed) or a prompt change ships that verifiably redirects logistics questions rather than answering them from the model's own general knowledge.
- STATUS: Backlog

**[P2] Build official roster: Louisiana (LA) US House — after Nov 3 open-primary qualifying closes**
- PARENT: c5a813bb-9223-4dc1-95aa-65637eb6940b
- ORIGIN: 2026-07-15, opened as part of Louisiana's own vertical-slice build per the epic's "NOT BEFORE DATE-GATE CONVENTION" standing requirement. Unlike every prior state's re-check card (a primary-stage roster waiting on certification to promote to general-ballot nominees), this one is a genuine BUILD, not a promotion: Louisiana's US House races use a new-for-2026 open ("jungle") primary on Nov 3, 2026 (Act 7 of the 2026 Regular Legislative Session), and candidate qualifying for that specific process had not yet opened at transcription time — the official qualifying period is August 5-7, 2026 (source: sos.la.gov/elections-voting/election-dates, confirmed 2026-07-15); a nominating-petition path closed July 9, 2026 but produced zero visible qualifiers as of this build. Live-checked the SoS Candidate Inquiry portal's own Nov 3, 2026 election-date view with every office selected: all 6 "U. S. Representative, Nth Congressional District" sections returned "No candidates." `la-official-roster-2026.ts` therefore has NO House entries and is registered in `FIXTURES` for `office: "senate"` only — see that file's docblock for the full reasoning, including why the Feb-2026-dated House filings visible under the portal's OLD closed-primary election-date view (5/16/2026) are stale/superseded by Act 7 and must NOT be used. Louisiana's US SENATE contest (Cassidy's Class 2 seat) IS fully determined already — closed primary (May 16) + runoff (June 27) both already past — and is NOT in scope for this follow-up card.
- OUTCOME: Louisiana's fixture gains a House roster (all 6 districts) built fresh from the post-qualifying official candidate list, registered in `FIXTURES["LA"]` alongside the existing Senate entry.
- IN SCOPE: re-check Louisiana's official source (`voterportal.sos.la.gov/CandidateInquiry?electionDate=20261103`, Statewide/Multi-Parish tab, all 6 House offices selected) after the Aug 5-7, 2026 qualifying period closes; transcribe every qualified House candidate into `la-official-roster-2026.ts`'s (new) `LA_HOUSE_ROSTER_2026` export, cross-checking incumbency against `house.gov`'s member directory (by name, not portal signal — Scalise/LA-1, Carter/LA-2, Higgins/LA-3, Johnson/LA-4, Fields/LA-6 are the current sitting members; LA-5's Letlow is running for Senate instead of re-election, an open seat); register the new House fixture in `FIXTURES["LA"]` in `scripts/ingest/official-roster.ts`; re-run the importer against the isolated staging Neon branch; check whether any race is undetermined pending the Dec 12, 2026 open-general runoff (`runoff_pending` per the epic's standard mechanism) — Louisiana's open-primary format means a Dec 12 runoff is the norm for any seat without a Nov 3 majority winner, not an edge case; also check Louisiana's candidate-withdrawal deadline for the newly-qualified House field per the epic's withdrawal-deadline standing requirement (general LA rule found this build: 4:30pm on the 9th day after the relevant primary date, or 4:30pm 2 days before early voting if exactly 2 candidates remain — La. R.S. 18:1400.1 per usvotefoundation.org, confirmed 2026-07-15; compute the exact date once the House field and any resulting runoff status are known); update `louisiana-vertical-slice-data-check.md` with the new candidate-by-candidate comparison; record any NEW governing calendar dates found and open a further `NOT BEFORE` follow-up card if the Dec 12 runoff leaves anything undetermined.
- OUT OF SCOPE: any other jurisdiction; production database writes/migrations; enabling `OFFICIAL_ROSTER_ENABLED` anywhere persistent; re-touching the already-determined Senate roster unless a re-check independently surfaces a withdrawal or correction.
- SAFETY: same as the original LA card — official-source reads only, no Ballotpedia as a primary source, never guess an undetermined nomination, cross-check incumbency independently against house.gov (never this app's own FEC-derived `candidates` table, and never the Feb-2026 stale closed-primary filings superseded by Act 7).
- GOAL_CONDITION: `npm run check` passes; LA's fixture gains a real House roster for all 6 districts; end-to-end verification against staging with the flag on shows correct candidate-by-candidate match for House (Senate unaffected); deliverable doc updated with the new comparison + any new governing dates.

If the portal's own "UNOFFICIAL until qualifying is closed" banner is still showing on re-check, wait a further day or two for the post-qualifying batch update before transcribing.

**[P2] Re-check official roster: Maine (ME) — US Senate DEM replacement nominee**
- PARENT: c5a813bb-9223-4dc1-95aa-65637eb6940b
- ORIGIN: 2026-07-15, opened as part of Maine's own vertical-slice build per the epic's "NOT BEFORE DATE-GATE CONVENTION" standing requirement. ME's fixture (`scripts/congressional-rosters/me-official-roster-2026.ts`, `docs/operations/maine-vertical-slice-data-check.md`) was built at `ME_STAGE = "general"` (Maine's June 9, 2026 primary was already past at build time) — every House row is a determined general-ballot nominee, but the US Senate contest carries only a single Republican row (incumbent Susan Collins). Graham Platner won the Democratic Senate primary outright (72.1%, no RCV needed) but formally withdrew on July 10, 2026, per the Secretary of State's own post-primary withdrawal document (corroborated by independent news reporting). Under 21-A MRS §374-A, the Maine Democratic Party has until 5 p.m., July 27, 2026 to file a replacement nominee (their nominating convention is scheduled for July 25); no replacement had been officially filed with the SoS as of this build's 2026-07-15 retrieval date, so no DEM row was guessed.
- OUTCOME: ME's Senate fixture reflects the Democratic Party's actual, officially-filed replacement nominee (or an explicit, documented continued vacancy if the party fails to file by the deadline).
- IN SCOPE: re-check Maine's official source (`maine.gov/sos/elections-voting`) for a filed Democratic replacement nominee for US Senate; add the nominee's row (`qualified_for_general_ballot`, `isIncumbent: false`) to `me-official-roster-2026.ts`'s `ME_SENATE_ROSTER_2026` once filed; if no replacement has been filed by 2026-07-27, 5 p.m., confirm that explicitly against the official source and record the continued vacancy (do not guess a name from convention-bid news coverage even if a single candidate appears to have won the party's internal process — wait for the SoS filing) and open a further short-dated follow-up card; also re-check for any newly-qualified independent/minor-party Senate filer since Maine's original non-party petition deadline (June 1, 2026) has passed but a late declared-write-in remains possible; re-run the importer against the isolated staging Neon branch; update `maine-vertical-slice-data-check.md` with the new candidate-by-candidate comparison; record any NEW governing calendar dates found.
- OUT OF SCOPE: any other jurisdiction; the two already-determined US House districts (no re-check needed unless a withdrawal is found — see the separate post-withdrawal-deadline sweep card below); production database writes/migrations; enabling `OFFICIAL_ROSTER_ENABLED` anywhere persistent.
- SAFETY: same as the original ME card — official-source reads only, no Ballotpedia/news coverage as a primary source for the nominee's identity (corroboration only), never guess an undetermined nomination, cross-check incumbency independently, never source from this app's own FEC-derived `candidates` table.
- GOAL_CONDITION: `npm run check` passes; ME's Senate fixture reflects either the officially-filed replacement nominee or an explicitly-documented continued vacancy (never a guessed name); end-to-end verification against staging with the flag on; deliverable doc updated with the new comparison + any new governing dates.
- STATUS: Backlog
- NOT BEFORE: 2026-07-28 — the day after Maine's statutory deadline (21-A MRS §374-A, 5 p.m. July 27, 2026) for the Democratic Party to file a replacement Senate nominee; checking exactly one day later gives the SoS's office time to post the filing (source: `maine.gov/sos/sites/maine.gov.sos/files/inline-files/2026%20Candidates%20Guide%20to%20Ballot%20Access%20Final.pdf`, confirmed 2026-07-15).
